do\n  if [ -f \\\"\"'$f\" ]; then echo \"FOUND $f\"; else echo \"MISS  $f\"; fi\ndone\nprintf '\"'\\\\n--- readme ---\\\\n'\nsed -n '1,220p' README.md\nprintf '\\\\n--- architecture ---\\\\n'\nsed -n '1,260p' site/content/docs/concepts/architecture.md\nprintf '\\\\n--- owners ---\\\\n'\nsed -n '1,220p' OWNERS\nprintf '\\\\n--- development ---\\\\n'\nsed -n '1,220p' site/content/contribute/development.md\nprintf '\\\\n--- trace grep ---\\\\n'\nrg -n \\\"NodePrepareResources|NodeUnprepareResources|Prepare|Unprepare|ResourceClaim|cdi|ComputeDomain|IMEX|channel\\\" cmd pkg api site deployments -S\"","aggregated_output":"# DRA Driver for NVIDIA GPUs\n\nEnables\n\n* flexible and powerful allocation and dynamic reconfiguration of GPUs as well as\n* allocation of ComputeDomains for robust and secure Multi-Node NVLink.\n\nFor Kubernetes 1.32 or newer, with Dynamic Resource Allocation (DRA) [enabled](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/#enabling-dynamic-resource-allocation).\n\n## Overview\n\nDRA is a novel concept in Kubernetes for flexibly requesting, configuring, and sharing specialized devices like GPUs.\nTo learn more about DRA in general, good starting points are: [Kubernetes docs](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/scheduling-eviction/dynamic-resource-allocation/), [GKE docs](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/about-dynamic-resource-allocation), [Kubernetes blog](https://kubernetes.io/blog/2025/05/01/kubernetes-v1-33-dra-updates/).\n\nMost importantly, DRA puts resource configuration and scheduling in the hands of 3rd-party vendors.\n\nThe DRA Driver for NVIDIA GPUs manages two types of resources: **GPUs** and **ComputeDomains**. Correspondingly, it contains two DRA kubelet plugins: [gpu-kubelet-plugin](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/main/cmd/gpu-kubelet-plugin), [compute-domain-kubelet-plugin](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/main/cmd/compute-domain-kubelet-plugin). Upon driver installation, each of these two parts can be enabled or disabled separately.\n\nThe two sections below provide a brief overview for each of these two parts of this DRA driver.\n\n### `ComputeDomain`s\n\nAn abstraction for robust and secure Multi-Node NVLink (MNNVL). Officially supported.\n\nAn individual `ComputeDomain` (CD) guarantees MNNVL-reachability between pods that are _in_ the CD, and secure isolation from other pods that are _not in_ the CD.\n\nIn terms of placement, a CD follows the workload. In terms of lifetime, a CD is ephemeral: its lifetime is bound to the lifetime of the consuming workload.\nFor more background on how `ComputeDomain`s facilitate orchestrating MNNVL workloads on Kubernetes (and on NVIDIA GB200 systems in particular), see [this](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1PrdDofsPFVJuZvcv-vtlI9n2eAh-YVf_fRQLIVmDwVY/edit?tab=t.0#heading=h.qkogm924v5so) doc and [this](https://docs.google.com/presentation/d/1Xupr8IZVAjs5bNFKJnYaK0LE7QWETnJjkz6KOfLu87E/edit?pli=1&slide=id.g28ac369118f_0_1647#slide=id.g28ac369118f_0_1647) slide deck.\nFor an outlook and specific plans for improvements, please refer to the [project releases](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/releases).\n\nIf you've heard about IMEX: this DRA driver orchestrates IMEX primitives (daemons, domains, channels) under the hood.\n\n### `GPU`s\n\nThe GPU allocation side of this DRA driver [will enable powerful features](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1BNWqgx_SmZDi-va_V31v3DnuVwYnF2EmN7D-O_fB6Oo) (such as dynamic allocation of MIG devices).\nTo learn about what we're planning to build, please have a look at the [project releases](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/releases).\n\nWhile some GPU allocation features can be tried out, they are not yet officially supported.\nHence, the GPU kubelet plugin is currently disabled by default in the Helm chart installation.\n\nFor exploration and demonstration purposes, see the \"demo\" section below, and also browse the `demo/specs/quickstart` directory in this repository.\n\n## Installation\n\nConfiguration and installation instructions are in the [documentation site](https://dra-driver-nvidia-gpu.sigs.k8s.io/docs/install/) ([source](site/content/docs/install.md)).\n\n## A (kind) demo\n\nBelow, we demonstrate a basic use case: sharing a single GPU across two containers running in the same Kubernetes pod.\n\n**Step 1: install dependencies**\n\nRunning this demo requires\n* kind (follow the official [installation docs](https://kind.sigs.k8s.io/docs/user/quick-start/#installation))\n* NVIDIA Container Toolkit & Runtime (follow a [previous version](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/blob/5a4717f1ea613ad47bafccb467582bf2425f20f1/README.md#demo) of this readme for setup instructions)\n\n**Step 2: create kind cluster with the DRA driver installed**\n\nStart by cloning this repository, and `cd`in into it:\n\n```console\ngit clone https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu.git\ncd dra-driver-nvidia-gpu\n```\n\nNext up, build this driver's container image and create a kind-based Kubernetes cluster:\n\n```console\nexport KIND_CLUSTER_NAME=\"kind-dra-1\"\n./demo/clusters/kind/build-dra-driver-gpu.sh\n./demo/clusters/kind/create-cluster.sh\n```\n\nNow you can install the DRA driver's Helm chart into the Kubernetes cluster:\n\n```console\n./demo/clusters/kind/install-dra-driver-gpu.sh\n```\n\n**Step 3: run workload**\n\nSubmit workload:\n\n```console\nkubectl apply -f ./demo/specs/quickstart/v1/gpu-test2.yaml\n```\n\nContainer log inspection then indeed reveals that both containers operate on the same GPU device:\n\n```bash\n$ kubectl logs pod -n gpu-test2 --all-containers --prefix\n[pod/pod/ctr0] GPU 0: NVIDIA A100-SXM4-40GB (UUID: GPU-4404041a-04cf-1ccf-9e70-f139a9b1e23c)\n[pod/pod/ctr1] GPU 0: NVIDIA A100-SXM4-40GB (UUID: GPU-4404041a-04cf-1ccf-9e70-f139a9b1e23c)\n```\n\n## Contributing\n\nContributions require a Developer Certificate of Origin (DCO, see [CONTRIBUTING.md](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/blob/main/CONTRIBUTING.md)).\n\n## Support\n\nPlease open an issue on the GitHub project for questions and for reporting problems.\nYour feedback is appreciated!\n\n--- architecture ---\n---\ntitle: Architecture\nlinkTitle: Architecture\nweight: 10\ndescription: DRA Driver components and request flows.\n---\n\nThe DRA Driver for NVIDIA GPUs delivers two independent Kubernetes DRA plugins:\n\n- `gpu.nvidia.com` - manages allocation of GPUs, including GPU sharing through MIG, Time-Slicing, and MPS, across both bare-metal and sandboxed environments.\n- `compute-domain.nvidia.com` - manages IMEX daemons and allocation of IMEX channel devices required for GPU memory sharing across OS domains on Multi-Node NVLink systems.\n\nBoth are delivered by the Helm chart in [`deployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/{{< param driver_release_tag >}}/deployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu).\n\n## Components\n\n| Binary | Runs as | What it does |\n|---|---|---|\n| `gpu-kubelet-plugin` | DaemonSet, per node | Publishes GPU / MIG / VFIO `ResourceSlice`s and injects CDI on Prepare. |\n| `compute-domain-kubelet-plugin` | Same DaemonSet, per node | Publishes IMEX daemon + channel devices and injects the IMEX mount on Prepare. |\n| `compute-domain-controller` | Cluster Deployment | Watches `ComputeDomain` CRs; spawns a per-CD DaemonSet and the matching `ResourceClaimTemplate`s. |\n| `compute-domain-daemon` | Per-CD DaemonSet, per node | Wraps and supervises `nvidia-imex`; reports peers. |\n| `webhook` | Cluster Deployment | Validates opaque config on `ResourceClaim`s. |\n\n```mermaid\ngraph TB\n    subgraph cluster[\"Cluster-scoped (Deployment)\"]\n        controller[\"compute-domain-controller\"]\n        webhook[\"webhook\"]\n    end\n    subgraph node[\"Each GPU Node (DaemonSet)\"]\n        gpu[\"gpu-kubelet-plugin\"]\n        cdp[\"compute-domain-kubelet-plugin\"]\n    end\n    subgraph percd[\"Per ComputeDomain (DaemonSet)\"]\n        daemon[\"compute-domain-daemon\"]\n    end\n    controller -->|spawns| percd\n    controller -->|creates ResourceClaimTemplates| cdp\n```\n\n## GPU request flow\n\nPod → `ResourceClaim` with a `GpuConfig` / `MigDeviceConfig` / `VfioDeviceConfig` → webhook validates → scheduler binds a device advertised by the GPU plugin → kubelet calls Prepare → plugin writes a CDI spec → runtime injects the GPU into the container.\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant User\n    participant API as Kubernetes API\n    participant Webhook\n    participant Scheduler\n    participant Kubelet\n    participant Plugin as gpu-kubelet-plugin\n    participant Runtime as Container Runtime\n\n    User->>API: Create Pod + ResourceClaim (GpuConfig)\n    API->>Webhook: Validate opaque config\n    Webhook-->>API: Approved\n    API->>Scheduler: Bind device from ResourceSlice\n    Scheduler-->>API: Node + device assigned\n    API->>Kubelet: Start Pod\n    Kubelet->>Plugin: Prepare(ResourceClaim)\n    Plugin-->>Kubelet: CDI spec written\n    Kubelet->>Runtime: Inject GPU via CDI\n    Runtime-->>User: Container running with GPU\n```\n\n## Driver-managed ComputeDomain flow\n\nUser creates a `ComputeDomain` → controller creates a per-CD DaemonSet → each daemon pod runs `nvidia-imex` → daemons publish their IP and clique through `ComputeDomainClique` CRs → workload pods claim a channel from the `compute-domain-default-channel.nvidia.com` DeviceClass → the CD kubelet plugin asserts readiness and injects one or more selected `/dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channelN` devices into the container.\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant User\n    participant API as Kubernetes API\n    participant Controller as compute-domain-controller\n    participant Daemon as compute-domain-daemon\n    participant Plugin as compute-domain-kubelet-plugin\n    participant Runtime as Container Runtime\n\n    User->>API: Create ComputeDomain\n    API->>Controller: Notify (watch)\n    Controller->>API: Spawn per-CD DaemonSet + ResourceClaimTemplates\n    API->>Daemon: Start on each GPU node\n    Daemon->>API: Publish IP + clique via ComputeDomainClique CR\n    User->>API: Create Pod claiming channel\n    API->>Plugin: Prepare(channel ResourceClaim)\n    Plugin-->>API: Assert readiness\n    Plugin->>Runtime: Inject selected IMEX channel devices\n    Runtime-->>User: Container running with NVLink fabric\n```\n\n## Reference\n\nFor opaque config types and CRD definitions: [API reference](../reference/api/)\n\nFor available feature flags and their defaults: [Feature gates](../reference/feature-gates/)\n\nFor Helm configuration: [Helm chart values](../reference/helm-values/)\n\nFor DeviceClasses shipped by the chart: [`deviceclass-*.yaml`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/{{< param driver_release_tag >}}/deployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/templates)\n\nFor example workloads: [`demo/specs/quickstart/`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/{{< param driver_release_tag >}}/demo/specs/quickstart)\n\n--- owners ---\n# See the OWNERS docs at Build, test, and the local-cluster iteration loop.\n---\n\n**Tooling:** Go (version per [`go.mod`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/blob/main/go.mod)), Docker + Buildx, Helm v3, kind, golangci-lint. Codegen tools are installed by `make -C deployments/devel install-tools`.\n\n## Make targets\n\n```\nmake build         # go build ./...\nmake cmds          # build the five binaries in cmd/\nmake test          # race-enabled unit tests\nmake generate      # CRDs, deepcopy, clientset, listers, informers\nmake check         # golangci-lint + check-generate  (the CI gate)\nmake helm-lint     # lint the Helm chart\nmake bats          # BATS integration tests  (needs a live cluster)\n```\n\nPrefix any target with `docker-` to run it in the devel container (requires `BUILD_DEVEL_IMAGE=yes`).\n\n## Local cluster\n\n```sh\nexport KIND_CLUSTER_NAME=kind-dra-1\n./demo/clusters/kind/build-dra-driver-gpu.sh     # build & load driver image\n./demo/clusters/kind/create-cluster.sh           # create the cluster\n./demo/clusters/kind/install-dra-driver-gpu.sh   # helm install\n```\n\nIterate: edit → rerun the build script → rerun the install script.\n\nGPU-aware variant: [`demo/clusters/nvkind/`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/main/demo/clusters/nvkind). GKE recipes: [`demo/clusters/gke/`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/main/demo/clusters/gke).\n\n## Tests\n\n- **Unit**: `make test`.\n- **BATS**: [`tests/bats/`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/main/tests/bats) — invasive, runs against a real cluster. Read [`tests/bats/README.md`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/blob/main/tests/bats/README.md) first.\n\n## CI\n\nGitHub Actions in [`.github/workflows/`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/tree/main/.github/workflows) run lint, codegen check, unit tests, build, chart lint, and CodeQL. End-to-end GPU tests run on Prow / Lambda Cloud — see [`hack/ci/lambda/e2e-test.sh`](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/blob/main/hack/ci/lambda/e2e-test.sh).\n--- trace grep ---\ncmd/gpu-kubelet-plugin/main.go:56:\tcdiRoot                       string\ncmd/gpu-kubelet-plugin/main.go:112:\t\t\tName:        \"cdi-root\",\ncmd/gpu-kubelet-plugin/main.go:114:\t\t\tValue:       \"/etc/cdi\",\ncmd/gpu-kubelet-plugin/main.go:115:\t\t\tDestination: &flags.cdiRoot,\ncmd/gpu-kubelet-plugin/main.go:141:\t\t\tName:        \"nvidia-cdi-hook-path\",\ncmd/gpu-kubelet-plugin/main.go:142:\t\t\tUsage:       \"Absolute path to the nvidia-cdi-hook executable in the host file system. **ComputeDomains** (`compute-domain.nvidia.com`) — provisions ephemeral multi-node NVLink fabrics using IMEX, enabling pods on different nodes to share GPU memory at full NVLink bandwidth.\nsite/content/docs/_index.md:22:DRA is a replacement for the device plugin architecture itself, not just a different driver for the same interface. It brings hardware resource management closer to the Persistent Volume model: a workload declares what it needs in a `ResourceClaim`, and the driver fulfills it. This separation of declaration from consumption enables capabilities that are fundamentally out of scope for the device plugin framework:\nsite/content/docs/_index.md:35:Request GPUs, MIG slices, or VFIO passthrough devices in a `ResourceClaim`.\nsite/content/docs/_index.md:39:### Multi-node NVLink with ComputeDomains\nsite/content/docs/_index.md:41:Create a `ComputeDomain` resource to provision an ephemeral NVLink fabric across nodes.\nsite/content/docs/_index.md:42:Workload pods claim a channel from the domain and receive the IMEX device mounts needed for direct cross-node GPU memory access.\nsite/content/docs/_index.md:52:- [API reference](reference/api/) — opaque config types for `ResourceClaim` specs (release {{< param driver_release_tag >}}).\ndeployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/values.yaml:25:# Optional path to the nvidia-cdi-hook executable.\ndeployments/helm/dra-driver-nvidia-gpu/values.yaml:30:# containers. You do not create or modify these directly.\nsite/content/docs/reference/api.md:169:Each `ComputeDomainClique` is namespaced to the driver namespace and named as `<computeDomainUID>.<cliqueID>`. ComputeDomains\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:5:description: How compute-domain.nvidia.com provisions ephemeral Multi-Node NVLink fabrics via IMEX.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:8:A `ComputeDomain` is a custom resource that sets up a group of nodes to run a multi-node workload using NVLink fabric. It is used to enable GPU memory sharing across nodes in hardware that supports Multi-Node NVLink (MNNVL), such as GB200 NVL72 or H100 NVLink configurations.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:12:## IMEX lifecycle modes\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:18:| `driverManaged` | By default, the DRA Driver creates one `nvidia-imex` DaemonSet for each `ComputeDomain` and tracks daemon readiness through `ComputeDomainClique` resources. |\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:19:| `hostManaged` | You run `nvidia-imex` as a host service with a ready command socket, and the DRA Driver does not create daemon DaemonSets or daemon claims; this mode requires the `HostManagedIMEXDaemon` feature gate. |\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:23:Creating a `ComputeDomain` in the default `driverManaged` mode triggers the following sequence:\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:25:1. The `compute-domain-controller` watches for new `ComputeDomain` resources and creates a per-domain DaemonSet.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:27:3. Each daemon publishes its IP address, clique membership, and readiness via a `ComputeDomainClique` CR in the driver namespace.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:28:4. The `compute-domain-controller` also creates a `ResourceClaimTemplate` per channel, making IMEX channels available for workload pods to claim.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:29:5. When a workload pod claims a channel, the `compute-domain-kubelet-plugin` injects one or more selected IMEX channel devices (`/dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channelN`) into the container.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:31:For the full sequence diagram, see [Architecture › Driver-managed ComputeDomain flow](architecture.md#driver-managed-computedomain-flow).\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:35:Creating a `ComputeDomain` in `hostManaged` mode uses an existing host `nvidia-imex` service:\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:38:2. The `compute-domain-controller` creates the workload `ResourceClaimTemplate`.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:39:3. When a workload pod claims a channel, the `compute-domain-kubelet-plugin` queries the host daemon through the daemon's command socket and requires a `READY` response. If the daemon is unavailable or not ready, claim preparation fails and is retried.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:40:4. After the readiness check succeeds, the plugin injects channel 0 (`/dev/nvidia-caps-imex-channels/channel0`) into the workload container.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:41:5. Deleting the `ComputeDomain` removes the workload claim template, but it does not stop or reconfigure the host service.\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:43:Host-managed mode currently supports domain isolation only. All ComputeDomains\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:44:that use the same host IMEX domain share channel 0. Because this mode has no\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:45:per-ComputeDomain daemon pods, the controller disables its\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:46:`IMEXDaemonsWithDNSNames` and `ComputeDomainCliques` behaviors and does not create\nsite/content/docs/concepts/compute-domains.md:47:`ComputeDomainClique` objects. For clusters where the operator already runs `nvidia-imex` as a host service (for example through systemd), set `resources.computeDomains.imex.mode=hostManaged` to stop the driver from creating per-ComputeDomain `nvidia-imex` DaemonSets. This **inverts** the requirement above: `nvidia-imex.service` must be installed, configured, and left **running** on every participating GPU node. Also, the `nvidia-imex` service should be configured with\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:76:`IMEX_CMD_ENABLED=1` and `IMEX_CMD_UNIX_DOMAIN_PATH=/etc/nvidia-imex/imex_ctrl.sock` (configurable via Helm).\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:80:For these units, set `IMEX_CMD_UNIX_DOMAIN_PATH` and `resources.computeDomains.imex.hostSocketPath` to the same writable runtime path, such as `/var/run/nvidia-imex-ctrl.sock`.\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:86:--set featureGates.HostManagedIMEXDaemon=true \\\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:90:- `featureGates.HostManagedIMEXDaemon` is an alpha gate that only unlocks setting `imex.mode=hostManaged`. It does not by itself change any behavior. Setting `imex.mode=hostManaged` without this gate enabled is a startup validation error (`helm install`/`helm template` fails immediately, and if bypassed, the controller and kubelet plugin pods will fail to start).\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:91:- `imex.isolation` selects the IMEX isolation strategy, and applies under both `driverManaged` and `hostManaged` mode. It must be set to one of:\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:92:  - `domain` (default) — all workloads running in the same IMEX domain share the same channel (0). Under `driverManaged` mode this is inherent to the model (the driver creates one `nvidia-imex` daemon per ComputeDomain); under `hostManaged` mode, multiple ComputeDomains can still run against the same host IMEX domain, all receiving channel 0, with no isolation between them.\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:93:  - `channel` — intended to eventually give each workload a unique channel within an IMEX domain. Not implemented yet: setting it is a startup validation error regardless of `imex.mode`.\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:95:- Changing `imex.mode` or `imex.isolation` on a cluster with active ComputeDomain workloads is not supported and is not enforced by the driver, drain and remove existing ComputeDomains first.\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:97:#### Host IMEX readiness check (Unix domain socket required)\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:99:Because there is no driver-managed daemon to report readiness under host-managed IMEX, the kubelet plugin validates the host `nvidia-imex` daemon itself at channel claim prepare time: it runs `nvidia-imex-ctl -q --u=<socket path>` chrooted into the driver root and requires a `READY` response before handing out a channel. A claim fails, and is retried, rather than succeeding silently, if the host daemon isn't reachable or ready.\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:106:   IMEX_CMD_ENABLED=1\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:107:   IMEX_CMD_UNIX_DOMAIN_PATH=/etc/nvidia-imex/imex_ctrl.sock\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:110:   then restart `nvidia-imex.service` so it creates the socket file (IMEX config changes require a restart to take effect).\nsite/content/docs/prerequisites.md:112:3.
